ኣብርሃምን ይስሓቕን

ድሕሪ እዚ ነገራት ኣምላኽ ንኣብርሃም ፈተኖ። «ኣብርሃም!» በሎ። ንሱ ድማ «እኔኹ» በለ።

ኣምላኽ ከኣ «ንወድኻ ንበይንኻ ንይስሓቕ እትፈትዎ ውሰድ ናብ ሃገር ሞርያ ኺድ ኣብኡ ኣብ ሓደ ካብቶም ኣኽራን ዝነግረካ መስዋእቲ ምሕራር ኣዕርገዮ» በለ።

ኣብርሃም ንጽብሒቱ ኣንጊሁ ተንስአ ዕንጸይቲ ሰቒሉ ምስ ይስሓቕ ወዱን ክልተ ገላዩን ከደ። ኣብ ሳልሳይ መዓልቲ ነቲ ቦታ ርእዩ።

ይስሓቅ ነቦኡ «ኣቦይ! ሓውን ዕንጸይትን ኣሎ ግን በጊዕ መስዋእቲ ኣበይ ኣሎ?» በሎ። ኣብርሃም ድማ «ኣምላኽ ባዕሉ በጊዕ መስዋእቲ ክቕርብ እዩ ወደይ» በለ።

ኣብርሃም መሰውኢ ሰርሐ ንይስሓቅ ኣሰረ ኣብ መሰውኢ ኣንበሮ ኢዱ ዘርጊሑ ካራ ሓዘ። ግን መልኣኽ እግዚኣብሄር ካብ ሰማይ ጸወዖ: «ኣብርሃም! ኢድካ ኣብ ልዕሊ ወድኻ ኣይተልዕል! ንኣምላኽ ከም እትፈርሆ ሕጂ ፈሊጠ!»

ኣብርሃም ቅር ኢሉ ድቢ ብቐርኑ ኣብ ጸምቢ ተታሒዙ ረኣየ። ነቲ ድቢ ኣብ ክንዲ ወዱ ኣዕረገ። ነቲ ቦታ «እግዚኣብሄር ይርኢ» ኢሉ ሰመዮ — ኣምላኽ ኩሉ ግዜ ይቕርብ!

Decorative Tigrigna text panel with Ethiopian/Eritrean border designs, Ge'ez script beautifully displayed with ram and mountain motifs. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 25 of 25

💝 The Moral of the Story

English: God tests our faith not to hurt us but to strengthen us and show us what's in our hearts. When we trust God completely — even when we don't understand His plan — He always provides! Abraham's willingness to give everything pointed to God's amazing love: He gave His own Son Jesus so we could be saved. God is Jehovah Jireh — the Lord who provides!

ትግርኛ: ኣምላኽ ንእምነትና ዝፍትን ንኸጕድኣና ኣይኮነን — ከደልድለናን ኣብ ልብና ዘሎ ክርኢን እዩ። ንኣምላኽ ምሉእ ብምሉእ ምስ እንኣምኖ — ትልሚ ኣምላኽ ኣብ ዘይንርድኣሉ እዋን እውን — ኩሉ ግዜ ይቕርብ! ኣምላኽ ንወዱ የሱስ ሂቡና ክንድሕን — ንሱ እዩ የሆዋ ይርኤ — እቲ ዝቕርብ ጐይታ!

Key Virtues: 🙏 Faith | 💪 Obedience | ❤️ Trust | 🐑 God's Provision | ✝️ Sacrifice

A beautiful moral summary scene showing a ram, an altar, and God's providing hands from heaven, with stars and sand representing Abraham's descendants. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
18

🗼 The Tower of Babel

ግምቢ ባቤል
📖 Genesis 11:1-9 | ዘፍጥረት 11:1-9
Scene 1 of 25

After the great flood, Noah's family grew and grew. His children had children, and their children had children! Over many years, the earth was filling with people again, just as God had commanded. And here's something special — in those early days, the whole world spoke ONE language! Everyone could understand everyone else perfectly.

A growing community of people after the flood, all talking and working together happily, speaking the same language, with Noah's ark visible on a distant mountain. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 2 of 25

As the people multiplied, they traveled together eastward and found a wide, flat plain in the land of Shinar. It was perfect for settling! The land was fertile, the rivers provided water, and there was plenty of space. "This is the place!" they said. "Let's build our homes here!" And so they settled and began their new life together.

A large group of people arriving at a vast flat plain between two rivers, setting up camps and beginning to build simple structures together. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 3 of 25

But then someone had a dangerous idea. Instead of spreading across the earth as God commanded, they said to each other: "Come! Let us build ourselves a city, and a TOWER whose top reaches into heaven!" They wanted to build the tallest, greatest structure the world had ever seen — one that would touch the very sky!

People gathered in excited discussion, pointing upward to the sky, someone drawing plans in the dirt showing a massive tower reaching to the clouds. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 4 of 25

Why did they want to build this tower? Pride! They said: "Let us make a NAME for ourselves!" They didn't want to honor God — they wanted to honor themselves! They wanted to be famous, powerful, and independent from God. They thought, "We don't need God! We can reach heaven by our OWN strength!" This was the sin of pride.

Proud-looking leaders with puffed-out chests boasting and pointing to themselves, imagining their great fame and power, with greedy expressions. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 5 of 25

They also said: "Let us not be scattered over the face of the earth!" But God had clearly told people to fill the earth — to spread out to every corner! They were directly disobeying God's command. They wanted to stay together in one powerful group, building their own glory instead of following God's plan for them.

People huddling together refusing to spread out, with arrows showing God's command to fill the earth being ignored, people shaking their heads 'no'. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 6 of 25

The construction began! They discovered how to make bricks from clay and bake them hard in fire. They used sticky tar as mortar to hold everything together. "Come, let us make bricks and burn them thoroughly!" they said. Thousands of workers began making bricks — stacking them, carrying them, building higher and higher every single day.

Workers making mud bricks, firing them in kilns, carrying bricks on their heads and backs, the beginning of construction with organized teams working together. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 7 of 25

The tower grew taller and taller! Day after day, week after week, month after month, more bricks were added. Ramps were built to carry materials higher. The tower rose above the houses, above the trees, above everything! The people looked up at their creation and felt so proud. "Look how great WE are!" they boasted to each other.

A massive ziggurat tower growing taller, with ramps and workers climbing up, the tower dominating the landscape, people looking up proudly at their work. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 8 of 25

Higher and higher they built! "We're almost touching heaven!" they laughed. "Soon we'll be as great as God Himself!" Their hearts swelled with arrogance. They forgot who gave them the intelligence to build. They forgot who created the very earth they stood on. They were like ants shaking their tiny fists at the sky, thinking they were giants!

The tower reaching into clouds, workers at the very top looking proud and arrogant, shaking fists at the sky, the tower casting a huge shadow over the land. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 9 of 25

But God saw everything. The Bible says the LORD "came down" to see the city and the tower that the people were building. Think about that! They thought their tower was reaching heaven, but God had to come down just to see it! Compared to God's greatness, even the tallest tower is like a tiny anthill. Nothing humans build impresses God.

God's perspective from high above showing the tower looking tiny on the vast earth, divine light looking down at the small construction, emphasizing God's greatness. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 10 of 25

God said: "Look! The people are united, and they all have one language. This is only the beginning of what they will do. Nothing they plan will be impossible for them!" God saw that if proud, sinful humanity stayed together with one language and one purpose against Him, they would only grow more wicked and bring more harm to themselves.

God observing the unified but rebellious people from above, seeing their combined strength being used for pride rather than good, with concern for their wellbeing. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 11 of 25

So God did something extraordinary! He said: "Come, let Us go down and confuse their language, so that they will not understand one another's speech!" And just like that — in one miraculous moment — God scrambled their languages! People who had been talking perfectly suddenly couldn't understand a single word their neighbor was saying!

A divine burst of colorful light coming down from heaven onto the tower and city, with swirling colors representing different languages being created. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 12 of 25

Imagine the confusion! One moment you're talking to your friend, the next moment — nothing makes sense! A worker shouts for more bricks, but his partner hears only strange sounds. A foreman gives orders, but everyone stares blankly. People opened their mouths and completely different languages came out! It was total chaos!

Workers on the tower suddenly confused, mouths open with different colored speech bubbles showing different languages, people looking bewildered at each other. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 13 of 25

Construction came to a complete halt! How can you build a tower when nobody understands each other? One person asks for a brick and gets handed water! Someone points up but their partner goes down! Arguments broke out everywhere — not because people were angry, but because they simply couldn't communicate! The great project was ruined!

Total construction chaos - workers handing wrong materials, pointing in different directions, bricks falling, people arguing and gesturing wildly in confusion. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 14 of 25

People became frustrated and upset. They waved their arms trying to explain things. They drew pictures in the dirt. They made hand signals. But it was no use — the confusion was too great! Families and friends who could still understand each other began grouping together, moving away from those who spoke differently.

People separating into small groups based on shared language, drawing pictures trying to communicate, some looking sad and frustrated, others forming new communities. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 15 of 25

Slowly, the different language groups began to scatter — moving away from each other and spreading across the land! Some went north, some south, some east, some west. Exactly what God had originally commanded was now happening — people were filling the earth! God's plan cannot be stopped, no matter how hard people try to resist it.

Groups of people walking in different directions away from the unfinished tower, carrying their belongings, spreading out to the four corners of the earth. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 16 of 25

Each language group traveled far and settled in new lands. They built new cities, developed new cultures, and their languages grew even more different over time. This is how the nations of the earth were formed! Every different language you hear today — Tigrigna, English, Arabic, Amharic, and hundreds more — all trace back to this one incredible moment at Babel!

Different groups settling in various landscapes - mountains, coasts, forests, deserts - developing unique cultures, with diverse languages represented by colorful text. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 17 of 25

The city was named "Babel" — which sounds like the Hebrew word for "confusion." What was meant to be a monument to human greatness became instead a reminder of human foolishness and pride. The unfinished tower stood as a crumbling lesson: when we try to build our lives without God, everything falls apart.

The abandoned, crumbling Tower of Babel standing alone, weeds growing on it, no workers, with the word "BABEL" written in crumbling letters, a lonely desolate scene. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 18 of 25

This story teaches us the danger of PRIDE. The people of Babel said "Let US make a name for OURselves!" They left God out completely. But the Bible says: "God opposes the proud but gives grace to the humble." When we think we don't need God, when we think we're the greatest — that's when we're in the most danger of falling!

A visual comparison: on one side a proud person standing on a crumbling tower, on the other a humble person kneeling before God being lifted up. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 19 of 25

But here's the beautiful ending to this story! Many years later, on the day of Pentecost, God reversed what happened at Babel! The Holy Spirit came and people from every nation heard God's message in their own language! What pride divided, God's love brought back together! Through Jesus, people from every language and nation can be united as one family!

Flames of the Holy Spirit descending on people from many nations, all understanding each other, joyful faces of diverse people united together in praise. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 20 of 25

The lesson of Babel versus the lesson of Pentecost: When humans try to reach God through their own effort and pride, there is only confusion and division. But when God reaches down to us through His Spirit, there is unity, understanding, and love! We can't build our way to heaven — but God came DOWN to us through Jesus!

A split scene: left side showing the tower crumbling with confused people, right side showing Jesus with arms open and people from all nations coming together joyfully. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 21 of 25

God's action at Babel wasn't cruel — it was merciful! If proud humans had stayed united in their rebellion, they would have destroyed themselves with even greater wickedness. God scattered them to protect them and to fulfill His good plan for the earth. Sometimes when God says "no" to our plans, He's actually saying "yes" to something better!

God's gentle hands redirecting people to beautiful different lands, like a loving parent guiding children to better paths, with flowers blooming where people settle. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 22 of 25

Today, let's remember: humility is strength! True greatness doesn't come from building towers to make ourselves famous. True greatness comes from serving God and serving others. The people of Babel are forgotten, but humble servants of God — like Abraham, Moses, and Jesus — are remembered forever! Choose humility, and God will lift you up!

A humble child helping others, being lifted up by God's light, contrasted with a crumbling tower in the background, showing true greatness through service. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 23 of 25

🌟 Let's Think About It!

Questions to consider:

  • Why did the people want to build a tower to heaven? What was wrong with their motivation?
  • What does "making a name for ourselves" mean? Do people still do this today?
  • Why did God confuse their languages? Was it punishment or protection?
  • How many languages can you name? Isn't it amazing how many there are?
  • What's the difference between pride and confidence? How can we stay humble?

Remember: We can't reach God by our own efforts — but God reached down to us through Jesus! Humility opens the door that pride keeps locked.

Children counting and naming different languages on their fingers, with the tower in the background and a rainbow of languages overhead. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 24 of 25

📖 ትግርኛ - Tigrigna Translation

ግምቢ ባቤል

ድሕሪ ማይ ኣይሂ ኩሉ ዓለም ሓደ ቋንቋ ይዛረብ ነበረ። ሰባት ንምብራቕ ተጓዒዞም ኣብ ጎልጎል ሺንዓር ተቐመጡ።

ንሓድሕዶም ከኣ «ንዑ ከተማ ንስራሕ ግምቢ ኸኣ ርእሱ ሰማይ ዝበጽሕ ንስራሕ! ስም ንገብር! ኣብ ልዕሊ ገጽ ኩሉ ምድሪ ከይንፋሰስ!» ተባሃሃሉ።

ጡብ ጌሮም ሰርሑ ግምቢ ከኣ ደኣ ክስርሑ ጀመሩ ልዕልን ልዕልን ኮይኑ! ኩሉ ግዝያት ንገዛእ ርእሶም ከመጐሱ ደለዩ።

እግዚኣብሄር ግን ነታ ኸተማን ነቲ ግምብን ክርኢ ወረደ። «እንሆ ሓደ ህዝቢ ኮይኖም ሓደ ቋንቋ ኣለዎም ክገብሩ ዝሓሰቡ ዘበለ ዘይከኣሎም የልቦን» በለ።

«ንዑ ንውረድ ቋንቋኦም ንደባልቖ ሓድሕዶም ከይሰማምዑ» በለ እግዚኣብሄር። ብድንገት ቋንቋኦም ተደባሊቑ ሓድሕዶም ክሰማምዑ ሰኣኑ!

ምስራሕ ኣቋረጹ ብኹሉ ምድሪ ተፋነው። ስም እታ ኸተማ ባቤል ተባህለት — ምኽንያቱ ኣብኣ እግዚኣብሄር ቋንቋ ኩሉ ምድሪ ደባሊቑ። ትዕቢት ናብ ምድብላቕ ትመርሕ ትሕትና ግን ናብ ክብሪ!

Decorative Tigrigna text panel with Ethiopian/Eritrean border designs, Ge'ez script beautifully displayed with tower and scattered people motifs. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 25 of 25

💝 The Moral of the Story

English: Pride goes before a fall! When we try to make ourselves great without God, we end up confused and scattered. True unity and greatness come only when we humbly follow God's plan. We cannot reach heaven by our own effort — but through Jesus, God reached down to us! Humility is the key that opens the door to God's blessings.

ትግርኛ: ትዕቢት ቅድሚ ውድቀት ትቕድም! ብዘይ ኣምላኽ ንገዛእ ርእስና ዓበይቲ ክንገብር ምስ እንፍትን ብምድብላቕን ብምፍልላይን ንውዳእ። ሓቀኛ ሓድነትን ዕቤትን ብትሕትና ትልሚ ኣምላኽ ምስ እንስዕብ ጥራይ እዩ ዝመጽእ። ብገዛእ ጻዕርና ሰማይ ክንበጽሕ ኣይንኽእልን — ግን ብየሱስ ኣምላኽ ናባና ወሪዱ! ትሕትና እቲ መኽፈቲ በረኸት ኣምላኽ እዩ።

Key Virtues: 🙏 Humility | 👂 Obedience | 🤝 Unity in God | ⚠️ Danger of Pride | 🌍 God's Sovereignty

A beautiful moral summary showing a humble person being lifted by God versus a proud tower crumbling, with the globe showing many nations united through love. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
19

👁️ Jesus Heals Blind Bartimaeus

ዓይነ-ስዉር ባርጢሜዎስ
📖 Mark 10:46-52 | ማርቆስ 10:46-52
Scene 1 of 25

On the dusty roadside outside the city of Jericho sat a man named Bartimaeus. He was completely blind — his world was nothing but darkness, day and night. He could hear the sounds of people passing, feel the warmth of the sun, and smell the market spices, but he could never see any of it. He lived in a world of endless night.

A blind man named Bartimaeus sitting by a dusty road with a beggar's cloak, empty eyes, reaching out his hand, people walking past him. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 2 of 25

Every day was the same for Bartimaeus. He would sit by the road, hold out his hand, and beg for coins from passersby. "Please, a coin for a blind man..." Some people dropped a small coin. Most walked past without even looking at him. He was invisible to them — just another beggar, forgotten and overlooked. Life seemed hopeless.

Bartimaeus begging with an outstretched bowl, most people walking past ignoring him, one person dropping a small coin, showing his daily lonely existence. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 3 of 25

But Bartimaeus had one thing that his eyes couldn't give him — he had EARS that listened! He had heard stories about a man named Jesus who could heal the sick and make blind people see. Day after day, sitting by the road, Bartimaeus heard travelers talking about Jesus's miracles. And deep in his heart, faith began to grow like a tiny seed.

Bartimaeus sitting with his ear turned toward travelers who are talking excitedly about Jesus, a small light glowing in his heart area showing growing faith. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 4 of 25

Then one day, Bartimaeus heard something different — a large crowd approaching! The ground vibrated with many footsteps. Voices buzzed with excitement. "What's happening?" he asked urgently. Someone answered: "Jesus of Nazareth is passing by!" Bartimaeus's heart exploded with hope! Jesus was HERE! This was his chance — maybe his ONLY chance!

Bartimaeus perking up alertly, hearing the approaching crowd, someone telling him about Jesus, his face lighting up with sudden hope and urgency. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 5 of 25

Bartimaeus didn't hesitate for even one second! He opened his mouth and shouted with all his might: "JESUS! Son of David! HAVE MERCY ON ME!" His voice cut through the noise of the crowd like a trumpet! He called Jesus "Son of David" — the title of the promised Messiah! Blind Bartimaeus could see who Jesus truly was, even without eyes!

Bartimaeus shouting with all his strength, mouth wide open, arms raised toward where he hears the crowd, voice lines showing how loud he's yelling. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 6 of 25

But the people around him were annoyed! "Be QUIET!" they scolded him harshly. "Stop yelling! You're just a beggar — Jesus doesn't have time for you!" They shushed him and told him to stop making a scene. Many people tried to silence him, telling him he wasn't important enough for Jesus's attention. But Bartimaeus refused to be silenced!

Angry crowd members shushing Bartimaeus, putting fingers to lips, waving hands dismissively at him, trying to quiet the persistent blind beggar. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 7 of 25

Instead of getting quiet, Bartimaeus shouted EVEN LOUDER! "SON OF DAVID! HAVE MERCY ON ME!!" He didn't care what people thought! He didn't care if they were angry! This was his ONE chance to be healed, and nothing was going to stop him! His desperate faith pushed past every obstacle. He screamed until his throat was raw!

Bartimaeus shouting even louder, veins showing in his neck from the effort, the crowd pulling back surprised by his intensity, his faith visible in his determined expression. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 8 of 25

Then something wonderful happened — Jesus STOPPED! In the middle of His journey, surrounded by hundreds of people, Jesus heard that one desperate voice and He stopped. Jesus said: "Call him here." Two simple words that changed everything! The crowd's attitude instantly shifted. "Cheer up! Get up! He's calling YOU!" they told Bartimaeus.

Jesus stopping in his tracks, turning toward Bartimaeus's voice, pointing and saying 'Call him', the crowd suddenly changing their attitude and becoming encouraging. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 9 of 25

What Bartimaeus did next shows his incredible faith! He threw off his beggar's cloak — the most important thing he owned, his blanket, his shelter, his identity — and he jumped to his feet! He threw away his old life in an instant! He wouldn't need that beggar's cloak anymore! He stumbled forward toward the voice of Jesus, arms outstretched!

Bartimaeus throwing off his cloak dramatically into the air, leaping up from the ground, stumbling forward eagerly with arms reaching out toward Jesus. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 10 of 25

Bartimaeus made his way through the crowd, guided by helping hands now, until he stood right in front of Jesus. He couldn't see Jesus's face, but he could feel His presence — warm, powerful, loving. The crowd fell silent. This was a holy moment. A blind beggar standing before the Son of God. Heaven was watching.

Bartimaeus standing before Jesus, still blind but face turned upward toward Jesus's voice, the crowd forming a circle around them in hushed anticipation. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 11 of 25

Then Jesus asked the most beautiful question: "What do you want Me to do for you?" Think about that! The God of the universe asking a blind beggar what HE wanted! Jesus already knew, of course. But He wanted Bartimaeus to speak his faith out loud, to declare his need with his own words. Jesus honors our specific, bold prayers!

Jesus bending down slightly to be at eye level with Bartimaeus, asking with genuine care, his hands gentle, Bartimaeus's blind face turning toward Jesus's voice. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 12 of 25

Bartimaeus spoke from the depths of his heart with trembling voice: "Rabboni! Teacher! I want to SEE!" Not money. Not food. Not a better begging spot. He wanted the one thing that seemed impossible — sight! He had lived in darkness his entire life, and he dared to ask for the one miracle that would change everything forever.

Bartimaeus speaking his heart's deepest desire with intense emotion, tears running down his face, hands clasped together pleadingly before Jesus. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 13 of 25

Jesus smiled with pure love and said: "Go — your FAITH has healed you!" Not Jesus's power alone — though He is all-powerful — but Bartimaeus's faith that reached out and grabbed hold of that power! Faith was the hand that received the gift. And in that INSTANT — faster than a blink — the miracle happened!

Jesus speaking the healing words with his hand reaching toward Bartimaeus's eyes, golden light flowing from his fingertips, a moment of divine power. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 14 of 25

IMMEDIATELY Bartimaeus could SEE! Light flooded into his eyes for the first time! Colors! Shapes! Faces! The world burst into view like a flower opening! After a lifetime of complete darkness — suddenly there was EVERYTHING! Blue sky! Green trees! The golden sun! And the FIRST THING his brand-new eyes saw was the most beautiful sight of all...

Bartimaeus's eyes flying open wide in shock and wonder, brilliant light flooding in, a burst of color and light around his head showing the moment of healing. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 15 of 25

The very first thing Bartimaeus saw with his brand new eyes was the face of JESUS! What a first sight! Those kind, loving eyes looking back at him. That gentle smile. The face of his Healer, his Savior, his Lord! Bartimaeus fell to his knees weeping with joy, unable to stop looking at Jesus. "I can SEE! I can SEE YOU!" he cried.

Bartimaeus seeing Jesus's face for the first time, overwhelmed with emotion, falling to his knees in worship, tears of joy streaming, Jesus's loving face filling his vision. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 16 of 25

Bartimaeus leaped up and immediately began following Jesus down the road! He didn't go home. He didn't go collect his beggar's cloak. He didn't run to show his friends. No — he followed Jesus! Praising God with every step, shouting hallelujah, dancing on the road he used to sit beside! His legs that had been still now danced with joy!

Bartimaeus joyfully following Jesus down the road, practically dancing, arms raised praising God, looking at everything around him in wonder for the first time. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 17 of 25

The entire crowd was amazed! People who had tried to silence Bartimaeus now stared in wonder! They had seen the miracle with their own eyes — a man born blind, now seeing perfectly! They began praising God too! "Did you see that? He was BLIND! Now he can SEE!" The joy spread through the crowd like fire, and everyone glorified God!

The amazed crowd celebrating, pointing at Bartimaeus who is seeing and dancing, people praising God with raised hands, expressions of wonder and joy everywhere. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 18 of 25

Bartimaeus looked at everything with wonder! The blue sky he'd never seen — "So THAT'S what blue looks like!" Flowers by the road — "How beautiful!" Children's faces — "So THAT'S what a smile looks like!" Everything that people take for granted every day was a brand-new miracle to Bartimaeus. The world was more beautiful than he had ever imagined!

Bartimaeus marveling at ordinary beautiful things - flowers, sky, children's faces, trees - with wonder and tears of joy, seeing the world for the very first time. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 19 of 25

What made Bartimaeus's healing happen? PERSISTENCE and FAITH! The crowd said "Be quiet!" — he got louder. People said "You're not important!" — he shouted more. Obstacles said "Give up!" — he pressed on harder. Bartimaeus teaches us that when we need God, we should never stop calling out to Him, no matter who tries to discourage us!

A visual of Bartimaeus breaking through barriers represented as walls labeled 'doubt', 'rejection', 'silence', with his persistent faith shown as light breaking through. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 20 of 25

Notice also what Bartimaeus threw away — his cloak! That cloak was his security, his identity as a beggar. By throwing it off, he was saying: "I won't need this anymore! I believe Jesus will heal me!" Sometimes we need to throw off our old identity — our old excuses, our old habits, our old doubts — and run to Jesus with nothing holding us back!

The discarded beggar's cloak on the ground, with Bartimaeus walking away from it toward Jesus in the bright sunlight, symbolizing leaving the old life behind. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 21 of 25

Jesus said "Your faith has healed you." This is powerful! Faith isn't just believing God can do something — it's believing He will! Bartimaeus didn't just think "maybe" — he KNEW! He threw off his cloak BEFORE he was healed! He acted on his faith before he saw the result. That's the kind of bold faith that moves God's heart!

A child stepping forward boldly in faith, with the word 'FAITH' as a bridge over a gap, showing confident trust before seeing the answer. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 22 of 25

And remember — Bartimaeus followed Jesus after being healed! He didn't just take the miracle and run away. He dedicated his new sight, his new life, to following the One who healed him. When God does amazing things for us, the best response is to follow Him with our whole lives — using every gift He gives us to glorify Him!

Bartimaeus walking behind Jesus as a devoted follower, looking at Jesus with grateful adoring eyes, other disciples around them on the road. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 23 of 25

🌟 Let's Think About It!

Questions to consider:

  • Why didn't Bartimaeus stop shouting when people told him to be quiet?
  • What does it mean that his "faith" healed him? How did he show faith?
  • Why did Bartimaeus throw off his cloak before he was healed?
  • What is something you need to persistently pray about?
  • What "cloak" (old habit or doubt) might you need to throw off to run to Jesus?

Remember: Jesus always stops for those who call out to Him in faith! Never let anyone tell you that you're not important enough for God's attention!

Children with eyes closed praying persistently, with visual of Jesus stopping and turning toward them, warm encouraging light. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.

📖 ትግርኛ - Tigrigna Translation

ዓይነ-ስዉር ባርጢሜዎስ

ኣብ ጥቓ ኢያሪኮ ሓደ ዓይነ-ስዉር ለማኒ ባርጢሜዎስ ዝስሙ ኣብ ጥቓ መንገዲ ተቐሚጡ ነበረ። ኩሉ መዓልቲ ይልምን ኣብ ጸልማት ይነብር ነበረ።

ሓደ መዓልቲ ብዙሕ ህዝቢ ክሓልፍ ሰምዐ። «እንታይ ኣሎ?» ኢሉ ሓተተ። «የሱስ ናዝራዊ ይሓልፍ ኣሎ!» ተባህለ።

ባርጢሜዎስ ብዓውታ ጸውዐ: «የሱስ ወዲ ዳዊት ራሕርሃለይ!» ህዝቢ «ስቕ በል!» ኢሎም ገንሕዎ። ግን ንሱ ኣዝዩ ደጊሙ ጨርሐ: «ወዲ ዳዊት ራሕርሃለይ!!»

የሱስ ደው ኢሉ «ጸውዕዎ» በለ። ንባርጢሜዎስ «ተባራታዕ! ተንስእ! ይጽውዓካ ኣሎ!» በልዎ። ባርጢሜዎስ ክዳኑ ደርብዩ ዘሊሉ ተንስአ ናብ የሱስ መጸ!

የሱስ «እንታይ ክገብረልካ ትደሊ?» ኢሉ ሓተቶ። ባርጢሜዎስ «ረቢ! ክርኢ ደልየ!» በለ።

የሱስ «ኪድ! እምነትካ ኣሕወየትካ!» በሎ። ሽዑ ንሽዑ ባርጢሜዎስ ረኣየ! ቀዳማይ ዝረኣዮ ገጽ የሱስ ነበረ! ንኣምላኽ እናመስገነ ንየሱስ ሰዓቦ!

Decorative Tigrigna text panel with Ethiopian/Eritrean border designs, Ge'ez script beautifully displayed with eye and light motifs. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 25 of 25

💝 The Moral of the Story

English: Never give up calling out to God! Bartimaeus didn't let the crowd's rejection stop him — he shouted louder! His persistent, bold faith moved Jesus to stop and heal him. When you need God, cry out with all your heart and don't let anyone silence you. Jesus always hears and always stops for those who truly seek Him in faith. Your faith can move mountains!

ትግርኛ: ናብ ኣምላኽ ምጥራዕ ኣይትሕመቑ! ባርጢሜዎስ ህዝቢ ስቕ ክብሉ ኣይሰምዖምን — ኣዝዩ ጨርሐ! ጽኑዕን ደፋርን እምነቱ ንየሱስ ደው ኣቢሉ ኣሕወዮ። ንኣምላኽ ምስ ዘድልየካ ብምሉእ ልብኻ ጸውዕ ሓድ እውን ከስቅተካ ኣይትፍቀድ። የሱስ ኩሉ ግዜ ይሰምዕ ንዝደልይዎ ብእምነት ደው ይብል! እምነትካ ኣኽራን ክትንቀሳቕስ ይኽእል!

Key Virtues: 🔊 Persistence | 💪 Bold Faith | 👁️ Spiritual Sight | 🏃 Action | 🙏 Following Jesus

A beautiful moral summary showing blind eyes opening to light, a discarded cloak, and a person running toward Jesus with arms wide open. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
20

🙏 Jesus Heals Ten Lepers

የሱስ 10 ለምጻማት ይፍውስ
📖 Luke 17:11-19 | ሉቃስ 17:11-19
Scene 1 of 25

Jesus was traveling on His way to Jerusalem, walking through the border region between Samaria and Galilee. His disciples walked with Him, and they passed through small villages along the dusty roads. Jesus knew what awaited Him in Jerusalem — the cross — but He continued healing and helping people every step of the way.

Jesus and his disciples walking along a dusty road between two regions, with small villages visible in the distance, a determined yet compassionate look on Jesus's face. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 2 of 25

As Jesus entered one village, He was met by a heartbreaking sight — TEN men with leprosy! Leprosy was a terrible skin disease that caused horrible sores, rotting flesh, and terrible pain. But the worst part wasn't the physical suffering — it was the loneliness. Lepers were forced to live completely alone, cut off from everyone they loved.

Ten men with visible skin disease standing together in a group at the edge of a village, wearing torn ragged clothes, looking sick and desperate. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 3 of 25

The law said lepers must stand far away from healthy people. They couldn't hug their children. They couldn't sit with their families for meals. They couldn't enter the synagogue. Whenever anyone came near, they had to shout: "Unclean! Unclean!" warning people to stay away. Can you imagine how lonely and sad that must have been?

The ten lepers standing far from the village, some reaching longingly toward houses where their families live, a gap of empty space between them and healthy people. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 4 of 25

These ten sick men saw Jesus coming and they cried out together from a distance — they didn't dare come close! They lifted their voices as loudly as they could: "Jesus! Master! HAVE MERCY ON US!" Their voices cracked with desperation. They had heard about Jesus's healing power, and this was their only hope. Ten voices, one desperate plea!

The ten lepers calling out from a distance, arms raised, mouths open in desperate cries, Jesus visible on the road turning to look at them with compassion. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 5 of 25

Jesus looked at them with deep compassion. He saw past their diseased skin to their broken hearts. He saw ten men who had been rejected by society, who had lost everything — their jobs, their families, their dignity. His heart was moved with love for each one of them. Jesus never looks away from suffering. He always looks toward it with love.

Jesus looking at the ten lepers with deep compassion in his eyes, tears glistening, his heart visibly moved by their suffering and desperation. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 6 of 25

Then Jesus called out to them with a simple but strange instruction: "Go! Show yourselves to the priests!" This was the law — only a priest could officially declare a leper clean and healed. But wait — they weren't healed yet! Their skin was still covered in sores! Jesus was asking them to act in faith BEFORE they could see the miracle!

Jesus calling out instructions to the lepers from the road, pointing toward the direction of the temple, the ten men looking at each other uncertainly. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 7 of 25

This took incredible faith! Would you start walking to the priest while you were still sick? But all ten of them chose to trust Jesus's word! They turned and began walking toward the priests, still covered in leprosy, still in pain — but obeying Jesus's command. Each step was a step of faith. Each step said, "I trust You, Jesus!"

The ten lepers beginning to walk away obediently, still visibly sick, looking uncertain but choosing to trust, taking their first steps of faith down the road. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 8 of 25

And then — AS THEY WALKED — the miracle happened! One man looked at his hand and gasped — the sores were disappearing! New, healthy skin was appearing! Another touched his face — smooth and clean! "Look! LOOK!" they shouted to each other. One by one, all ten were being completely healed! Their rotting flesh became as fresh as a baby's skin!

The lepers mid-walk suddenly noticing their skin changing, looking at their hands in amazement, sores vanishing and healthy new skin appearing, expressions of shock and joy. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 9 of 25

Their skin became completely clean! Not a single sore remained! The disease that had stolen their lives, their families, their dignity — gone! In one miraculous moment, ten men who had been outcasts were made perfectly whole! They could go home! They could hug their children! They could rejoin their communities! It was the most wonderful feeling in the world!

All ten men with completely clean, healthy skin, examining their hands and arms in wonder, some crying with joy, some laughing, all amazed at their transformation. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 10 of 25

All ten realized they were healed! Joy exploded among them! They shouted, they laughed, they cried, they danced! "We're HEALED! We're CLEAN! We can go HOME!" The excitement was overwhelming. All ten were overjoyed. But then something sad happened — nine of them kept running forward toward the priest and their families, without looking back.

Nine of the healed men running excitedly toward the village, arms waving, rushing toward their homes and families, with one man stopping and turning back. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 11 of 25

Nine men ran away happily, thinking only about themselves — their families, their old lives, getting back to normal. They forgot the most important thing! They forgot to say THANK YOU to the One who healed them! They took the miracle and ran, without even a backward glance at Jesus. How quickly they forgot who gave them this incredible gift!

The nine men disappearing into the distance, running in different directions toward their homes, Jesus watching them go with a sad expression, only their backs visible. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 12 of 25

But ONE man stopped. He looked at his clean hands. He looked at the road ahead. Then he turned and looked back at Jesus. And something swelled up in his heart so powerfully that he couldn't keep walking away. Gratitude! Overwhelming, overflowing gratitude! He HAD to go back! He turned around and ran — not toward home, but toward JESUS!

One man stopping mid-stride, turning around with tears of gratitude, beginning to run back toward Jesus while the other nine continue forward without looking back. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 13 of 25

This man came running back to Jesus, praising God in a LOUD voice! "GLORY TO GOD! HALLELUJAH! PRAISE THE LORD!" He didn't care who heard him! He didn't care what people thought! His heart was so full of thankfulness that praise just poured out of him like a river! He praised God with every step back toward Jesus!

The grateful man running back toward Jesus with arms raised, shouting praises, his face radiant with joy and gratitude, golden light surrounding him. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 14 of 25

When he reached Jesus, he fell face down at Jesus's feet! Right there in the dust of the road, this healed man threw himself on the ground in complete worship and gratitude. "THANK YOU! Thank you, Master! Thank you!" he cried, his tears making the dust muddy. He kissed Jesus's feet and just kept repeating, "Thank you! Thank you!"

The grateful man prostrate at Jesus's feet, face in the dust, hands touching Jesus's feet, weeping with gratitude, Jesus looking down with loving approval. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 15 of 25

And here's an important detail — this grateful man was a Samaritan! Samaritans and Jews didn't get along at all. They were considered outsiders, foreigners, not part of God's people. Yet this "outsider" was the ONLY one who came back to thank Jesus! Sometimes the people we least expect show the most gratitude and faith!

The Samaritan man at Jesus's feet, with a subtle indication of his Samaritan identity through his clothing, disciples looking surprised that a Samaritan returned. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 16 of 25

Then Jesus asked a question that still echoes through time: "Were not TEN healed? Where are the other NINE?" Jesus looked around, but only one man was there thanking Him. He continued with sadness in His voice: "Was no one found to return and give glory to God except this foreigner?" The silence was deafening. Only one out of ten.

Jesus asking the question with open hands, looking at the empty road where nine men ran away, only the one grateful Samaritan at his feet, a look of sadness at the ingratitude. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 17 of 25

Think about those numbers: ten healed, only ONE returned to give thanks. That's only 10%! Is that how we treat God's blessings too? How often do we receive good things from God — health, food, family, friends — without ever stopping to say "Thank you, God!"? Are we like the nine who take and run, or like the one who stops to give thanks?

A visual showing the number 10 with 9 fading away and 1 highlighted and glowing, representing the ratio of thankful versus unthankful, with question marks around. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 18 of 25

Then Jesus said something special to the grateful man. He said: "Rise and go — your faith has made you WELL!" All ten were healed of leprosy, but this man received something more — he was made completely whole! Not just his body, but his soul! His gratitude opened the door to a deeper blessing that the other nine missed!

Jesus helping the grateful man to his feet, speaking a blessing over him, a special glow of wholeness around the man showing both physical and spiritual healing. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 19 of 25

The nine received healing of their skin, but the one who returned received healing of his whole being — body, mind, and spirit! Gratitude doesn't just bless God — it blesses US! When we thank God, our hearts stay connected to Him. The nine walked away healed but disconnected. The one walked away healed AND transformed — whole in every way!

A comparison showing the nine men healed physically but walking away alone, versus the one man glowing with wholeness - body, heart, and spirit all healed, connected to Jesus. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 20 of 25

This story teaches us the power of GRATITUDE! Saying "thank you" isn't just good manners — it's a way of worshiping God! Every good thing we have comes from God: every breath, every meal, every sunny day, every person who loves us. When we develop a grateful heart, we see God's blessings everywhere, and our joy multiplies!

A child surrounded by blessings - food, family, sunshine, friends - with hands clasped in thankful prayer, seeing all these gifts as coming from God above. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 21 of 25

Let's also notice: the nine weren't bad people. They were happy! They were going to follow Jesus's instruction and see the priests! But they forgot the most important thing — giving glory to God. Being busy isn't an excuse for being ungrateful. No matter how excited we are about God's blessings, we must always stop and thank the Giver!

Busy people rushing about their day, contrasted with one person stopping in the middle of busyness to kneel and pray thank you, showing the choice to be grateful. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 22 of 25

Today, let's be like the one who returned! Let's develop a habit of gratitude. When you wake up — "Thank you God for a new day!" When you eat — "Thank you for food!" When someone is kind — "Thank you for friends!" Fill your life with "thank yous" and watch how much more joy and blessing flows into your heart!

A child going through their day saying thank you at each moment - morning, meals, school, play - with little thank you bubbles and hearts floating up to heaven. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 23 of 25

🌟 Let's Think About It!

Questions to consider:

  • Why do you think only one out of ten came back to thank Jesus?
  • Are you more like the nine or the one? Do you remember to say thank you to God?
  • Why was it surprising that the Samaritan was the grateful one?
  • What are three things you can thank God for right now?
  • How does saying "thank you" change your heart and bring you closer to God?

Remember: Gratitude is the language of love! When we thank God, we stay connected to Him and open our hearts to even more blessings. Always say thank you!

Children making a gratitude list together, writing or drawing things they're thankful for, with Jesus smiling approvingly in the background. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 24 of 25

📖 ትግርኛ - Tigrigna Translation

የሱስ 10 ለምጻማት ይፍውስ

የሱስ ናብ ኢየሩሳሌም ክኸይድ ከሎ ኣብ መንጎ ሰማርያን ገሊላን ሓለፈ። ኣብ ሓንቲ ዓዲ ኣትዩ ከሎ ዓሰርተ ለምጻማት ሰባት ኣብ ርሑቕ ደው ኢሎም ጸንሕዎ።

ብዓውታ ድምጺ «የሱስ መምህር ራሕርሃልና!» ኢሎም ጨርሑ። የሱስ ብርሕራሐ ጠመቶም።

«ኪዱ ንኻህናት ርእስኹም ኣርእዩ!» በሎም። ብእምነት ተበጊሶም ኸዱ — ክኸዱ ከለው ተፈወሱ! ቆርበቶም ጽርይቲ ኮነት!

ዓሰርቲኦም ተፈወሱ ግን ሓደ ጥራይ ተመልሰ! ብዓውታ ንኣምላኽ እናመስገነ ናብ የሱስ ጎየየ። ኣብ ቅድሚ እግሩ ተደፊኡ «ኣመስግነካ!» በለ። ንሱ ሳምራዊ ነበረ!

የሱስ «ዓሰርተዶ ኣይኮኑን ዝጸረዩ? እቶም ትሽዓተ ኣበይ ኣለው? ካብዚ ጓና ሓሊፉ ንኣምላኽ ክብሪ ክህብ ዝተመልሰ ተረኺቡዶ?» በለ።

ነቲ ሰብኣይ ድማ «ተንስእ ኪድ! እምነትካ ኣሕወየትካ!» በሎ። ምስጋና ኩሉ ግዜ ንኣምላኽ ኣቕርቡ — ልቢ ምስጋና ዝመልኦ ልቢ በረኸት ዝመልኦ እዩ!

Decorative Tigrigna text panel with Ethiopian/Eritrean border designs, Ge'ez script beautifully displayed with hands in prayer and the number 10 motifs. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
Scene 25 of 25

💝 The Moral of the Story

English: Always say THANK YOU to God! Out of ten people healed, only one returned to give thanks — and he received an even greater blessing! Gratitude opens our hearts to deeper relationship with God. Don't be like the nine who took God's blessing and ran away. Be like the one who returned — stop, worship, and thank God for everything He has done! A grateful heart is the happiest heart!

ትግርኛ: ኩሉ ግዜ ንኣምላኽ ኣመስግኑ! ካብ ዓሰርተ ዝተፈውሱ ሓደ ጥራይ ተመሊሱ ኣመስገነ — ንሱ ኸኣ ኣዝዩ ዝዓበየ በረኸት ረኸበ! ምስጋና ልብና ንዕሙቕ ዝበለ ርክብ ምስ ኣምላኽ ይኸፍት። ከምቶም ትሽዓተ ኣይትኹኑ — ከምቲ ሓደ ኩኑ! ደው ኢልኩም ንኣምላኽ ብዘገበረልኩም ኩሉ ኣመስግኑ! ልቢ ምስጋና ዝመልኦ ኣዝዩ ሕጉስ ልቢ እዩ!

Key Virtues: 🙏 Gratitude | 💕 Worship | 🏃 Returning to God | ✨ Wholeness | 🌟 Thankfulness

A beautiful moral summary showing one person bowing in gratitude before God's light while blessings overflow, with the number 1 highlighted among 10 figures. East African/Eritrean features, watercolor children's Bible illustration.
